# 📨 Cross-Owner Requests

Format: `- [open|done] <from> → <owner>: <path>, <change>, <reason>`

- [done] A1 → A2: `✏️s/🔌️plugins/📕️norm/🎬️structural-scene/🧬️schema/{📈️diagram2d,🕹️handles}/🦀️.rs` (+ `{🛰️.proto,🔗️.graphql,🟦️.ts}` per dir), deliver the part files. A1's `🧬️schema/🦀️.rs` already mounts both with `#[path]` + `pub use *`. Start each file with `use super::*;` to get `records!`/`variants!`/`choices!` (camelCase value derives; adjacent `kind`/`data` tagging for `variants!`), `Validate`/`Violation` (implement `Validate::check(&self, path, &mut Vec<Violation>)`), helpers `at,nth,expect,finite,positive,non_negative,non_empty,each,TOLERANCE`, and types `Vec2,Vec3,Frame,EntityRef,LocalizedCopy,Quantity,QuantityKind,Aabb`. `handles` MUST export `FieldBinding` (A1's `Annotation::Dimension.binding: Option<crate::FieldBinding>` uses it); `HandleRef` in `SceneEntity` is a plain `String` handle id. Crate: `semio_s_plugin_norm_structural_scene`.
- [done] A1 → A2: the four top-level twins (`🧬️schema/{🛰️.proto,🔗️.graphql,🟦️.ts}`) import your part files: proto `import "📈️diagram2d/🛰️.proto"` / `import "🕹️handles/🛰️.proto"` (package `semio.norm.structuralscene`, reuse `Vec3`, `Frame`, `EntityRef`, `LocalizedCopy`, `Quantity` from the top-level file), TS `export * from "./📈️diagram2d/🟦️.ts"` / `"./🕹️handles/🟦️.ts"`, GraphQL `#import "📈️diagram2d/🔗️.graphql"` / `"🕹️handles/🔗️.graphql"` lines.

- [done] oracle-infra → all artifact agents (en1990-en1999): oracle tests MUST spawn Python through `semio_s_plugin_norm_compliance::python_oracle::{command(), run(script,&[args],stdin), run_ok(..)}` (feature `compliance-testing`, already a dev-dependency of every artifact schema/inferences crate). - [done] en1991 → compliance owner: `✏️s/🔌️plugins/📕️norm/⚖️compliance/✅️check-results/🦀️.rs`, `CheckBuilder::minimum` sets the utilisation of a failing check to `computed/minimum` (below 1) and `CheckSummary::worst_utilization` therefore hides failing rows; use `required/available` (above 1) or return the ratio inverted, and treat `limit <= 0` as Fail instead of utilisation 0 in `CheckBuilder::utilization`. ## NDP registry adoption (compliance owner -> ALL artifact agents)

- [open] ndp-registry → all artifact agents (EN 1990-1999): the kernel now owns the NDP registry. Do NOT keep private `UNVERIFIED` lists or `[V]` badges; cite registry ids instead. Recipe:
  1. Find your values in `📌️important/ndp-verification-worklist.md` (section 2, grouped by code; the id is the first column; section 1 lists the repo-vs-spec conflicts, verify those first).
  2. In the check body add `.ndp("ndp.en1992.v-02.1")` on the `CheckResult::assess(...)` builder (any number of calls, duplicates collapse; unknown ids panic in debug builds and count as unverified in release). If the value you use has no row yet, add one to `⚖️compliance/📜️annex-and-tables/🔖️ndp/🔣️.json` (keys: id, code, part, clause, symbol, recommended, naDe, unit, source{document,edition,locator}, status `unverified`, specRef, asWritten; keep ids sorted) and ask the compliance owner (or run `🧰️ndp/codegen.py <json> <🗃️table.rs>` from the ticket) to regenerate `🔖️ndp/🗃️table.rs`. Never hand-edit `🗃️table.rs`.
  3. Lookup values with `semio_s_plugin_norm_compliance::ndp(id)` (static, no allocation) and `entry.value(AnnexChoice)` returning `NdpValue::{Number,Text,Unset}`; keep the numeric literal in your logic only until the row carries a typed value.
  4. A `Pass` or `Warning` check that cites an unverified id is turned by `build()` into `CheckStatus::UnverifiedBasis` (explanation EN+DE lists the ids, utilisation is kept). `Fail` stays `Fail`. `CheckReport::complies()` is false while any check is `UnverifiedBasis`; `summary.unverified`, `PartVerdict.unverified`, `CheckReport::unverified()` and `CheckReport::unverified_ndps()` feed the UI badge. Do not special-case the status in checks; update your tests that expect `Pass` for checks that now cite unverified ids to expect `UnverifiedBasis`.
  5. Wire/UI: new `CheckResult.ndps: string[]`, summary and part `unverified: number`, and status `"UnverifiedBasis"` (schema twins updated in `⚖️compliance/🧬️schema`). TS results windows that compare `status` strings (`✏️editor/🎭️modes/✏️edit/🪟️windows/📊️results/🟦️.ts`) must render `UnverifiedBasis` as an amber "Unverified basis / Ungeprüfte Grundlage" chip; `app_surface::status_label` already does. Scene kits map it to `scene::CheckStatus::Warning` (done in en1992/1993/1994/1997/1998 kits); en1990/1991/1995/1996/1999 scenes must do the same when they add one.
  6. `CheckBuilder::minimum` failures now report utilisation `minimum/computed` (> 1, capped at `UTILIZATION_CAP = 1e6`), `utilization()` against a zero limit fails instead of passing, and `Fail` is always reported with utilisation >= 1; report `worst_utilization` is therefore >= 1 whenever a check fails.

Resolved: wave2/en1994.md:4 (editor mojibake repaired).

All modules are pure per-entity functions in `C/🧮️clauses` (mm, N, MPa; registered in `🧮️clauses/🦀️.rs`; tests `🧪️tests/🔭️special-parts/🦀️.rs`, standalone 26/26 green incl. oracle `S/🔮️oracles/🧮️special-parts/🐍️.py`). |---|---|---|
| V-250/251 1-3 distortional | `cold_formed::distortional_edge_stiffener(&EdgeStiffened{bp,cp,t,fyb,hw,b2,kf,e}, gm0)` -> `chi_d`, `a_s`; reduced `reduced_stiffener` [`en1993.1-3.5.5.<m>`, util = sigma_Ed/(chi_d fyb/gM0)] | **cold-formed section entity**: `bp, cp, t, hw, b2, kf` per flange (mm) |

Spec/discrepancy notes for the primary agent:
- `joints::k5_end_plate` uses 0.85; EN 1993-1-8 Table 6.11 has k4 = k5 = 0.9 (`joint_components::k4_column_flange`, `k5_end_plate_bending` use 0.9). Please re-point `joints::k5_end_plate` or delete it in favour of the new function (no duplicates).
- `plates_shells::patch_loading` covers only type (a); `plated_ext::patch_resistance` covers a, b and c with identical type (a) results; replace to avoid duplication.
- `misc::permissible_thickness` returns the last row for T_Ed below the table (non-conservative); `toughness::max_thickness` returns `None` there and the caller must treat `None` as failed (util > 1, remedy: higher sub-grade).
- Public API keeps only std/f64/&str/enums of this crate; no external types leak.

Build: ready for rebuild (see `🗑️generated/build-en1993/latest.md`): new modules compile warning-free standalone (`rustc --test`, nightly-2026-07-20); no snapshot/schema fields were changed by this agent.

## [mechanics] -> all artifact and visual agents: shared mechanics kernel `semio-s-plugin-norm-mechanics`

Crate `✏️s/🔌️plugins/📕️norm/🧮️mechanics` (workspace dependency `semio-s-plugin-norm-mechanics`, project `@semio-s/plugin-norm-mechanics`). Pure Rust, no runtime dependencies, SI, Z up, deterministic. Compile owner: mechanics agent / integrator. Report: `🔍️audits/mechanics-kernel.md`.

API (all via `use semio_s_plugin_norm_mechanics::*`, errors are `MechanicsError`, every long call takes `&Control` = progress callback + `AtomicBool` cancel):
- Model: `Model { nodes, materials, sections, members, supports, load_cases, masses, self_mass }`; `Model::refine(n)` splits each member in `n` elements (ids `member#k`, nodes `member@k`) for buckling, modal and mode-shape work.
- Statics: `let a = Analysis::new(&model, &control)?; a.solve_case(&case, &control)?` -> displacements, reactions, `MemberForces { end_i, end_j, diagram: Vec<DiagramPoint {x,n,vy,vz,t,my,mz}> }` (`a.stations` sets diagram samples). `a.member_curve(&shape, "member", samples)` -> real displaced centre line (Hermite) for any displacement field.
- EN 1993 alpha_cr: `a.buckling(&case, count, &control)` -> `BucklingMode { alpha_cr, shape }` (axial-force geometric stiffness of the case; use `refine(8..16)`).
- EN 1993 Mcr: `ltb::ltb_analyze(&LtbMember {..}, &control)` -> `alpha_cr`, `mcr = alpha_cr * max|M|`, mode shape `(x, lateral, twist)`. Arbitrary moment table (`uniform_moment`, `end_moments`, `uniform_load_moment`, `central_point_moment` or your own `(x, M)` points, e.g. from a static diagram), line/point loads with `load_height` (positive above the shear centre = destabilising), end/intermediate restraints (lateral, twist, minor rotation, warping), axial force, Wagner term.
- EN 1998 / EN 1995 / EN 1999 vibration: `a.modal(count, MassType::Consistent|Lumped, &control)` -> `omega`, `frequency_hz`, `participation`, `effective_mass`, `effective_mass_ratio` (global X, Y, Z), `shape` (max translation 1), `total_mass`. Set `Model::self_mass` and/or `NodalMass`.
- EN 1990 / 1991 / 1995 / 1999 mode shapes and internal forces: replace sinusoidal indications by `buckling`/`modal` shapes plus `member_curve`; replace simplified M/V/N by `solve_case` diagrams.
Limits: member-level Mcr ignores monosymmetric Wagner effects unless `wagner` is set; frame buckling uses axial geometric stiffness only (use `ltb_analyze` for LTB); models above 600 free DOF use skyline subspace iteration.

## Check stations (kernel `check-results` + projection-kit) -> integrator, ALL norm-completion agents (EN 1990-1999), ALL visual agents

- [open] check-stations → integrator: ready for rebuild of `@semio-tech/norm-compliance-rs` and `@semio-s/plugin-norm-structural-scene` (kit). New `Station`, `CheckResult.{stations, station_field}`, `CheckBuilder::{stations, field, sampled}`, `CheckResult::{max_station_utilization, stations_consistent}`; kit module `station_fields` (`station_values field_along segment_values field_at_points`), `binding_of_check` now fills `per_station`. Fixture/schema twins updated; run the TS fixture test (`check-report-fixture`) and the new `📍️check-stations` oracle too.
- [open] check-stations → norm-completion agents (compile note): `CheckResult` has two new fields. Only the builder constructs it (no struct literals exist outside the kernel), so nothing breaks; do not construct `CheckResult { .. }` literally.
- [open] check-stations → norm-completion agents EN 1990-1999: emit stations where the verification varies in space. Contract: `utilization == max(stations)` is enforced by `build()` (a station above 1.0 turns a Pass into a Fail, so attach a remedy). API: `CheckResult::assess(..).sampled(n, |s| (util, vec![governing quantities]))` for members (`s` in 0..=1, n+1 stations); `.stations(vec![Station::along(s, u).with(q)])`; `.field("soil-pressure", vec![Station::point(x, y, u)])` for plates, `Station::point3` for solids; `Station::ratio_of(&at, demand, capacity)` keeps demand and capacity. Skip `.utilization(..)` for a pure station check (computed and limit stay zero) or call it first for the governing pair. Wanted:
  - member checks along length (bending, shear, axial/buckling, LTB, combined N+M, deflection per position): EN 1992 6.1/6.2, EN 1993 6.2/6.3, EN 1994 6.2, EN 1995 6.1/6.3, EN 1999 6.2/6.3; field names empty (member `s`), use 11 stations (`sampled(10, ..)`) or the load-case critical sections; quantities `[demand, capacity]`.
  - walls (EN 1996 6.1.2 and 6.1.3): reduction factor Phi per height `s` (bottom, 1/4, mid, 3/4, top), field `wall-phi`, quantities `[N_Ed, N_Rd]` (member coordinate = height / h).
  - slab punching (EN 1992 6.4): one point per control-perimeter segment `(x, y)` in the column-local frame, field `punching-perimeter`, quantities `[v_Ed, v_Rd]`.
  - footings and bearing (EN 1997 6.5, 6.6): soil pressure at corners and mid-edges `(x, y)` of the footing, field `soil-pressure`, quantities `[q_Ed, q_Rd]` (bearing utilisation per point); slope and retaining slip surfaces keep the governing slice only.
  - drift (EN 1998 4.4.3.2 and EN 1990 A1.4): one station per storey, `s` = storey index / (storeys - 1), field `storey-drift`, quantities `[d_r v, limit]`.
  - fire/section fields (EN 1992/1994/1995/1999 -1-2): `section-temperature` points `(y, z)` in section-local coordinates where the verification samples them.
  - fatigue (EN 1993-1-9, EN 1992 6.8): per detail position `s` along the member.
  Keep stations compact (<= 64 per check, positions finite, member `s` in [0,1], utilisation finite and non-negative, `at` length 1..=3).
- [open] check-stations → visual agents: `semio_s_plugin_norm_structural_scene::projection_kit` (`scene::projection_kit`) consumes them. `binding_of_check(&check)` already sets `UtilisationBinding.per_station` for member stations (ascending, unique, max wins on ties). For vertex colours use `field_along(&check, &params)` (linear in `s`, clamped), for segmented members `segment_values(&check, n)` (max of ends and inner stations), for plates and solids `field_at_points(&check, &[[x, y, z]])` (inverse-distance weighting, exact at stations). `check.station_field` names the 2D/3D field to pick the legend. Use the governing check of a subject via `worst_of_subject`, then its stations; when `stations` is empty keep today's single colour from `utilization`.
- [open] check-stations → NDP registry agent: coordinated; your `ndps` edits in `check-results` were preserved (re-read before every edit).

- [done] vis-en1992 → schema-owner: `Pattern::Cage` is now `Cage { outline: Ring2, zones: Vec<LinkZone>, reference: Vec3 }` (any closed link shape, per-zone spacing, outline x along `reference` projected perpendicular to the axis, outline y along axis x that). `hole_clearance` is for bolts only now. EN 1992 `members::links` already switched (rectangular outline from section width/height, cover and link diameter) and its test reads `zones`/`outline`.
- [done] vis-en1991 → schema-owner: `SolidShape::WallAssembly` gained `openings: Vec<WallCut { offset, width, sill, height }>` (doors with `sill = 0` are notches, windows are holes, offsets run along the whole path); tessellation and viewport cut them; EN 1991 snow parapet passes `openings: Vec::new()`.
- [done] vis-en1995 → schema-owner: `SolidShape::Laminate { frame, footprint, layers: Vec<GrainLayer { thickness, role, grain, label }> }` stacks CLT/glulam layers upward from the frame origin with the fibre angle per layer (tessellated per layer, one part per role). EN 1995 owner: draw CLT with it once the snapshot has a layup.
- [done] vis-en1999 → schema-owner: `FastenerKind::WeldRun { throat, weld, path: Path3, pitch: Option<WeldPitch { length, gap }> }` is a weld along a path, continuous or intermittent (EN 1993 and EN 1999 joints can replace the straight `Weld` per plate edge). Section polygons with holes already exist as `SectionShape::Polygon { polygon: Polygon2 { outer, holes } }` (`Parametric` Chs/Rhs also emit holes): EN 1999 `outline`/`holes` request needs only a snapshot field and the scene switching to `Polygon`.

- [done] vis-en1997/en1992/en1998 → schema-owner: `ColourChannel::Scalar { field_id }` is replaced by `ColourChannel::Fields { active, fields: Vec<ScalarField { id, unit, samples }> }` with `FieldSamples::{Vertex, Station, Instance}`: several named scalar fields travel with the entity (settlement, tendon force P(x), stress, per-station moment) and `active` selects the displayed one. EN 1997 settlement surface switched (vertex values in metres). Viewport colours a flat entity by the mean of the active field (per-vertex ramping is the next step in `🎞️lowering`).
- [done] vis-en1990/en1993/en1995/en1998/en1999 → schema-owner: `DeformationOverlay` gained `surface: Vec<SurfaceSample { at: Vec2, delta: Vec3 }>` (plate/shell mode shapes in plane coordinates, `samples` may then be empty), `motion: Motion::{Static, Harmonic, Ramp}` and `load_factor: Option<f64>` (alpha_cr of a buckling mode). All 11 literals got `surface: Vec::new(), motion: …, load_factor: None` (Mode -> Harmonic, Deformed -> Static); owners should now fill the real eigenmode samples, frequency and alpha_cr.

EN 1998-1 4.3.3.2.4(2)/4.3.3.2.2: L_e and L_i are measured perpendicular to the seismic action. - [open] oracle → en1997: informational. `bearing_drained` with `include_depth` multiplies by a depth factor d_q; Annex D of EN 1997-1 gives none (it is a DIN 4017 / Hansen factor). Label it outside the Eurocode or remove it; cases `en1997.D.4.bearing-resistance-*` carry the Annex D values without depth factors and agree with the Rust factors.
- [open] oracle → en1990: informational. `KN_V_UNKNOWN` at n = 20 prints 1.76 (as in the table) while the Student prediction factor t_(19;0.95) sqrt(1 + 1/n) is 1.772; between table nodes the linear interpolation in 1/n is within 0.3 percent of the t form. Cases use n = 5, 10, 30 where table and formula agree to 0.2 percent.

- [announce] live-scene → all artifact owners and SCENE v3: scene modules now take an owner cursor. Rules when you add a visual: (1) every row loop starts with a guard (`if !out.wants(&row.id) { continue; }`, or `out.own("collection", &row.id)` in en1993/en1997/en1998/en1999, `out.enter(unit)` in en1991); (2) put the guard before building geometry, after any running totals; (3) tracks and legends that depend on the whole scene go into the kit `finish` (see `tracks::finish(scene)`), not into `project`; (4) if your visual reads another row, add its id to `entity_dependencies` and, for index/cursor-dependent poses, to `layout_stamps`; (5) read check rows only by subjects of the entity, its dependencies or `""`. The law test `🎬️scene/🧪️tests/🔬️live` fails when one of these is missed.

## from: compile-review C (wave 2)
- [open] compile-review C → coordinator (all ten artifact owners; structural, found by reading, NOT yet seen in a log because no build has compiled an `io` crate): the codec impls live in crates other than the crate that defines the type, which the orphan rule rejects. Per artifact: `🚪️io/📝️text/📸️snapshot` (`impl_norm_artifact_record!(@text …)` = `NormArtifactRecord` + `pack::snapshot::ArtifactDsl` for the model's `…Snapshot`), `🚪️io/💾️binary/📸️snapshot` (`@binary` = `ArtifactPack` + `ArtifactStoreSnapshot`), `🚪️io/🪶️sqlite/📸️snapshot` (inherent `impl …Snapshot {` = E0116, plus `ArtifactSqliteSnapshotHost`/`ArtifactSqliteSnapshot`), and `📝️text/🧬️mutations`, `💾️binary/🧬️mutations` (`protocol::OpText`/`OpBinary` for the mutations crate's `…Mutation`) = E0117. The framework's own pattern is the owner-mounts pattern: `🧊️generation3d` model crate does `#[path = "../🚪️io/📝️text/📸️snapshot/🪞️dsl-mirror/🦀️.rs"]` and `…/🔺️diff/🦀️.rs` inside the crate that owns `Generation3dSnapshot`, and the io crates only `pub use …model::diff_text as diff;` / `pub use …mutations::text as mutations;`. The norm model/mutations crates mount nothing from `🚪️io`. Expected first errors once diff compiles: E0117 in `semio-s-artifact-norm-enXXXX-io-text`, E0116/E0117 in `…-io`. Cheapest verification: after the shared crates and diff crates are green, the first `enXXXX-rs:build` that reaches io-text shows it; do not wait for ten builds, one artifact (en1990, smallest io) is enough. Fix shape: move the trait impls into the owner crates (mount the codec files via `#[path]` in the model crate resp. the mutations crate, add the `pack`/store deps those files use, keep the io crates as re-exporting facades), then apply to all fifteen artifacts mechanically. Needs a single owner (coordinator) so the ten trees do not diverge.
- [open] compile-review C → en1999 owner: `semio-s-artifact-norm-en1999-checks` is at the gate: 259934 B / 18 files / 4007 lines (limit 4000 lines, 262144 B; it was 268844 B / 4152 lines minutes earlier). Source admission fails the whole `en1999-rs:build` above 4000 lines. Move a sibling crate out before the next growth. (`en1990-diff` was 4065 lines minutes ago and is back under; `vdi3805-model` 4276 and `iso16757-model` 4433 lines are over but outside the ten.)
- [info] compile-review C fix: `app-surface/🪟️visual-windows/🧾️properties/🧪️tests/🔬️unit/🦀️.rs` `include_str!("../../🔮️oracles/🔣️.json")` resolved to a missing `🧾️properties/🔮️oracles`; now `../🔮️oracles/🔣️.json` (the oracle sits in `🧪️tests/🔮️oracles`). Affects only `panels:test`.

## [mechanics] section solver (wave 2): `semio_s_plugin_norm_mechanics::analyse_section`
- [announce] mechanics -> all artifacts: finite-element cross-section solver in the shared kernel (`🧮️mechanics/🔲️section`, nothing compiled). API: `analyse_section(outer: &[[f64;2]], holes: &[Vec<[f64;2]>], &Material) -> MechanicsResult<SectionConstants>`; `analyse_section_with(.., &SectionOptions, &Control)` adds mesh density (`cells_across_wall` default 4, `max_edge`, `max_triangles` 40 000) and progress (stages mesh, assemble, factorise, solve, integrate) plus `AtomicBool` cancellation; `analyse_thin_walled(&[ThinWall], &Material)` for open centreline walls. `SectionConstants`: area, centroid, iy (z squared), iz, iyz, i_major/i_minor, `j` (St Venant), `iw` (about the shear centre), shear centre (absolute y,z), shear areas `a_vy`/`a_vz`, elastic moduli `w_el_{y,z}_{pos,neg}`, plastic moduli `w_pl_y/z`, `to_section(id)` -> kernel beam `Section`. Profile outlines `section::profiles::{i_section, mono_i_section, channel_section, tee_section, angle_section, rectangular_hollow_section, elliptical_hollow_section, circular_hollow_section, rectangle}` follow the sectionproperties constructors (n points per quarter arc). Validated against sectionproperties in Python (rectangle, I, channel, angle, RHS, CHS, hexagon hole, IPE300, PFC200, L100x10, tee, plate girder, rounded RHS, EHS): J, I_w, shear areas within 0.1 percent in the prototype, test tolerance 0.5 percent; the shear centre to 1e-5 of the section size. Report `🔍️audits/wave2/mechanics.md`.
- [done] mechanics -> en1999 norm agent: `⚖️checks/🌀️section-solver` lost the raster solver (`solve`, `Solved`, its unit tests); the crate keeps `stability` (frames, Wagner, M_cr, N_cr,TF) and its tests. `🪢️free-outline-torsion::free_constants` now calls `analyse_section` (alloy E 70 GPa, G 27 GPa), `wall_cells` is the mesh-edge count across the mean wall (Warning below 3), the info text says finite element within 0.5 percent. `⚖️checks/📦️packages/🦀️rust/Cargo.toml` and the artifact `Cargo.lock` gained `semio-s-plugin-norm-mechanics`. The old 3-5 percent tolerances in your free-outline tests can be tightened (I_t, I_w and A_v now agree with sectionproperties to 0.5 percent, M_cr follows).
- [done] mechanics -> en1993 norm agents (v4 and VIDS): the 7 unwired checks plus the IPE300 row are wired in `⚖️checks/🧪️tests/🎯️oracle-wiring/🦀️.rs` (`kernel_section` with `profiles::*` and `analyse_section`; `en1993.section.{tee,mono-i,ehs,rhs,channel,angle}`, `en1993.6.3.2.mcr-i` through `stability::mcr_closed_form`; `rolled_i_catalogue` falls back to the kernel when no HEB row matches). Registry size 34. Please keep these adapters when you edit the file. For production (not only oracle) use add `semio-s-plugin-norm-mechanics` as a normal dependency and call `analyse_section(&outline.0, &outline.1, &material)`; `parametric_sections::Properties` could carry `it`, `iw`, `y_sc` from `SectionConstants` for tee/mono-i/ehs/rhs-rounded/channel/angle. The open third-party finding (HEB 240..300 catalogue `it`/`iw` wrong) stays with you: sectionproperties gives It 103.7/125.9/145.4/187.7 cm4 for HEB 240..300 (the kernel solver is validated against it, not yet run on these rows).
- [open] mechanics -> en1994 / en1999 / en1993 owners: warping constants of rolled and welded sections (`catalogue::ISection::iw()`, mono-i Iw) can now come from `analyse_section(&profiles::i_section(..))`; plastic moduli with fillets come from `w_pl_y/z`.

## from: en1996 artifact owner v4 (wave 2)
- [done] en1996 → ndp v3: the NDP tagging of `load-case-checks` and `fire-durability-and-reinforcement` now follows the data flow. Every state computation runs inside `record_ndp_reads` (tabulated K, alpha, beta, delta, k-e, f_vk0, f_xk and fire cells are cited from the kernel log), the f_k basis comes from `fk_basis` + `m01.delta` (`strength_ndps`), formula rows are cited only where the formula is used. Corrections: compression no longer cites `m14.beta` (concentrated load) and gains `m06.creep`, `m06.e`, `m10.rho2`, `m12.einit` and `m13.phi1` only for NA-DE; eccentricity drops `m13.phi1`; shear drops `m03.head-joint` (no model field) and the K row (it uses f_vk), gains `m20.stability`; concentrated gains `m14.beta`, the bearing-area ratio cites only `m14.beta`; flexure gains the f_k basis and `m40.flexure` when bed-joint steel is declared; fire is split: tabulated thickness cites `m90.fire` + the read cells only (no `m07.gamma`, no `m91.fire`), the load ratio cites f_k basis, gamma, `m06.e`, `m10.rho2`, `m12.einit`, `psi`; durability drops `m60.exposure` (frost/cover, not used); reinforced drops `m41/m42/m43/m45`; simplified drops `m82.shear`, gains f_k basis, `m10.rho2`, `m11.limit`, `m13.phi1` (DE); basement gains `m81.phis`, `m10.rho2` and the f_k basis. ## from: doc-oracles v2 (en1995/en1999)
- [done] en1999 -> en1999 owner: `en1999.6.1.6.haz.<run>` reports status `warning` (not `fail`) when the HAZ reach exceeds the element width, while its utilisation is above 1 (weld-1 on a 31 mm flange, 30 mm HAZ per side: reach 60 mm / 31 mm = 1.9355, status warning). The check-outcome oracle models this as `warning`; a feature row that expects `fail` on that id would disagree. Decide whether the status should follow the utilisation like every other row. (adjudicated: already fixed in the Rust, test `a_haz_wider_than_its_element_fails_by_the_shared_status_rule_and_never_warns`; the oracle models fail.)
- [open] en1999 -> compliance owner (oracle): the older oracle `⚖️compliance/🐍️.py` ignored `weldRuns[].hazExtent` in the section strips (Rust `haz_overrides` honours it); the new `🚦️check-outcome` oracle patches it (web run with hazExtent 20 mm). No Rust change needed; if a row on a run with `hazExtent > 0` fails after the rebuild, compare the strips first.
- [info] en1999 -> verification: the buckling row `en1999.6.3.1.haz.<member>` is modelled as the worst of `fb`/`fb.z` of the welded member (N_b,Rd,y or z already carries kappa and omega_x in the Rust n_b closure); fixtures: purlin-1 0.5717 (pass), 24.049 (fail, 5 m buckling length).
- [info] en1995 -> verification: design-case rows are `en1995.t-nn.<outcome>.<case id>` (Rust `format!("en1995.{entry}.{key}.{case.id}")`); the older oracle's `cat_t30` handled the compression edge only (the Rust default side is `tension`, k_m,alpha with 0.75 f_v,d and f_t,90,d); the new oracle covers both. Values (GL28h, EN): T-30 tension edge 0.3425 / 3.4253, compression edge 0.2629, T-32 0.6029 / 6.0291, T-45 0.8703 / 8.7028, T-46 0.4462 / 4.4616.
- [done] en1995 -> compliance owner: `size_factor_kh` returns 1.0 for hardwood solid classes (D-classes), the member-check oracle (`k_h`) has no hardwood branch, so a D-class member would disagree by the 3.2 size factor (<= 1.3). No fixture covers it. (adjudicated: the Rust was wrong, EN 1995-1-1 3.2(3) limits k_h by rho_k <= 700 kg/m3, not by wood type; `size_factor_kh` takes `dense = rho_k > 700`, test added; the oracle already applied k_h to solid timber.)
- [open] en1995 -> fixtures: the schema fields `nKN`, `mKNm`, `vKN`, `nTKN`, `fC90KN` of member actions are SI (N, N m), the `KN` in the names is misleading (a 30 kN axial force is `nKN: 30000`); rename after the kind convention (touches diff/mutation kinds).

## from: doc-oracles v2 (en1998)
- [open] en1998 -> en1998 owner (storey shears): `check_storey_forces` computes the accidental-torsion amplification `delta` (1.3 on the fixture, 1.6 simplified bound) and then drops it (`let _ = delta_torsion;`), so `storeyForces.<storey>`, `overturning` and `drift` use unamplified forces. EN 1998-1 4.3.3.2.4 multiplies the action effects by delta. Oracle (unamplified, as the Rust does) `seismic-rich-pass` s2 0.1574; with delta 1.3 it would be 0.2046.
- [open] en1998 -> en1998 owner (`planDims`): the check reports `utilization = Lx / Ly` (24/16 = 1.5 on every fixture) but forces status Pass; the shared step layer reads `pass_or_fail(1.5)` = fail. Set the utilisation to 0 for positive dimensions (or drop the utilisation); no `planDims` row in the case for that reason.
- [open] en1998 -> en1998 owner (`elevationRegularity`): the soft-storey and mass-variation rows are emitted only inside `if plan_width_m > 0 && plan_length_m > 0`; a building without plan dimensions silently loses its soft-storey check. Move the loop out of the block.
- [open] en1998 -> en1998 owner (`planRegularity`): `e0 = max(e0x, e0y)` is compared with `0.30 * min(rx, ry)`; EN 1998-1 4.2.3.2 compares e0x with 0.30 rx and e0y with 0.30 ry. Identical when kx = ky (all fixtures), conservative otherwise. Oracle uses the per-direction form: a document with kx != ky will differ.
- [open] en1998 -> old compliance oracle (`⚖️compliance/🐍️.py`, evaluated by the earlier case): EN annex branch used S = 1.2, TB 0.15, TC 0.40, TD 2.0 for every ground (Table 3.2 ground B is TC 0.50), ignored gamma_I (importance III: oracle 0.1292 vs Rust 0.1550), cut q at 1.0 instead of 1.5 and q_max, and never applied the 20 % elevation-irregularity reduction or the Rayleigh period. The new `🚦️check-outcome/🐍️.py` replaces it for the case; the Rust values read from the source agree with the new oracle (change-annex EN: 0.2108, importance III 0.1550, rayleigh 0.1050, elevation irregular 0.1615, wall type 0.1717).
- [info] en1998: `qLimit` = q / q_max sits exactly on 1.0 for the default fixture (q 5.85, q_max 5.85), no row is taken from it; design uses `min(q, q_max)`, so a q0 above the table raises `qLimit` but not the base shear.
- ready for rebuild: doc oracles v2 en1998 (new oracle, two fixtures, 24 new rows)

(2) `en1990.fat.<m>` is downgraded to Warning when > 1, so a failing fatigue range has status `warning` while the utilisation is > 1 (oracle fixture value 0.3333 kept as a pass row only); the same holds for `annex-b.dsl|il` (RC3 against DSL2: 1.5, Warning) and `table-2.1.design-working-life` (60 a in category 4: 1.2, Warning): `pass_or_fail(utilization)` reads these as `fail`, the Rust says `warning`; the case therefore holds no failing row for them. (3) `a14.deflection` subject path is `deflectionW3` for the net deflection (remedy writes `precamber`); cosmetic.

## from: en1992 v5 (wave 2)
- [open] en1992 -> scene owner: `FastenerKind::Rebar { bend }` bends only the `to` end, towards `perpendicular(direction)`; EN 1992 hooks need both ends (or `bend_start`) and a roll/side vector so the hook always turns into the section. The EN 1992 scene picks the one end whose bend side points into the section (`reinforcement::hook_end`) and skips the other.

## from: doc-oracles v2 (en1996/en1997)
- [open] en1996 -> owner (`6.2.shear`, `⚖️masonry/🧱️evaluate-wall/🏋️load-case-checks`): `inPlaneShearN` / `inPlaneMomentNm` of a wall never enter `shear()`: V_Ed is only `gamma_Q (0.5 w_k h L + H_earth)` from `design_effects`, so the mutations `change-in-plane-shear` and `change-in-plane-moment` leave `en1996.6.2.shear.*` at the same utilisation (wall-north 0.0931 before and after) and only the catalogue document cases read them (`📇️masonry-catalogue/🧮️document-cases`, shear walls only). A shear wall with 400 kN in-plane shear passes the document check. Fix: add the in-plane V_Ed (and the corresponding l_c / sigma_d) to the governing combination or emit a shear-wall row.
- [open] en1996 -> owner (`6.1.2.compression.<wall>.<lc>`): when Phi reaches 0 (eccentricity above t/2) N_Rd is floored at 1e-9 N and the utilisation is 6.6e14 to 7.4e14 (fixture `change-support-sides` wall-weak 6.61e14). The status is right, the number is a unit-less floor artefact; a capped value (for example 1e9, as the en1998 rows do) would keep the bar and badge scales usable.
- [info] en1996 oracle: the new `🚦️check-outcome/🐍️.py` agrees with the old snapshot oracle on the four old families; the six new families (eccentricity, shear, flexure, concentrated, area, per load case) have no known disagreement with the Rust by reading (same combination set max-N with imposed or snow leading, min-N with wind and earth leading; shear takes the combination with the largest V / N_mid, flexure the largest w; N_Rdc = beta f_d A_b with beta capped at min(1.25 + a1/2h, 1.5)). Not run on the Rust side.
- [open] en1997 -> owner (`9.earthPressure.<wall>`, `6.5.bearing` for geotechnical category 1): status-only rows without a utilisation (earth-pressure regime vs movement; category 1 skips bearing), so check-outcome has no numeric row for them. If a number is wanted (for example K_used / K_regime) it needs an owner decision.
- [done by correctness-2] en1997 earth pressure now has utilisation K_required/K_used (free: active, rigid/propped: increased active); conservative over-use passes, under-use fails; category-1 bearing already had V_d/R_d (presumptive table, now unverified registry rows `ndp.en1997.gk1.presumptive.*`, flagged); the GK1 gap was in the oracle (`continue`), fixed. bishop3d: oracle row added (SLOPE-05 is not in the spec, flagged).
- [info] en1997 oracle: the `🚦️check-outcome/🐍️.py` wraps the existing stdlib library (`📚️geo.py`), whose Nq and N_gamma are now validated against groundhog 0.15 (Nq to 1e-9; N_gamma EN = 2(Nq-1)tan phi = Vesic - 4 tan phi), and the Boussinesq stress under the rectangle centre against groundhog's Newmark corner solution (1e-3). No numeric disagreement with the Rust is known: the Rust could not be run. Rows to compare after the rebuild: the 19 new rows of `🚦️check-outcome-en1997-1`; slowest are the settlement and pair rows (the oracle takes 15 s per document, the dry run of the case about 8 minutes because the dry-run script does not cache per fixture).

## from: CATALOGUE-ORACLES (wave 2)
- [done] oracle vs Rust disagreements (oracle value from the standard text, Rust read, not run): (adjudicated, see `🔍️audits/wave2/adjudicator.md`: T-95 oracle fixed (k_f,1/k_f,2/k_f,3 from the registry, EN text read), E14.09 oracle fixed (interpolation, 7.2.9(5)), E4.01 registry rows + both sides strict boundaries, E2.13 registry band rows, E17.10 no disagreement (Mills on both sides, registry evidence), EN 1996 pass-through is a comparison-depth limit not a disagreement.)
  - EN 1995 T-95 (DE annex) `f_d`: oracle N_d/k_f,3 with the DIN EN 1995-1-1/NA k_f,3 = 30 (solid) / 50 (glulam, LVL) gives 666.7 N for N_d = 20 kN; Rust divides by `force-divisor-solid` 50 (EN recommended) gives 400 N. `q_d` agrees for DE (111.1 N/m, divisor 30). For annex EN the oracle uses 50 / 80 for both `q_d` and `f_d` (q_d 66.7 N/m against Rust 111.1 N/m with its fixed `stabilising-divisor` 30). The NDP rows `ndp.en1995.bracing.{stabilising-divisor,force-divisor-*}` need annex-specific values (owner: en1995). Recalled from the printed 9.2.5.3 (9.34), unverified: needs a check against the clause text before the Rust is changed.
  - EN 1991 E14.09 `c_pi_dominant`: oracle steps 0.75 c_pe (2 <= ratio < 3) / 0.90 c_pe (ratio >= 3); Rust interpolates linearly between ratio 2 and 3 (7.2.9 (6) names only the two values). The demo ratio 1.0 does not reach it.
  - EN 1991 E4.01 `aac`: oracle <100 t = 1, >10000 t = 3 (boundary values class 2); Rust `<=100` = 1, `<=10000` = 2. The demo (500 t) agrees.
  - EN 1991 E17.10 `p_so`: oracle uses Mills p = 1772/Z^3 - 114/Z^2 + 108/Z kPa (20 543.7 Pa at W = 100 kg, R = 30 m, identical to Rust); the Kinney-Graham fit gives 18 828.2 Pa (8.4 percent lower). The spec says Annex E is not used: confirm which fit the entry cites.
  - EN 1991 E2.13 `resonance`: encodes 2.0 / 0.0 against a limit of 1.0 (a warn outcome); EN 1990 A2.4.3.2 bands 5 Hz / 2.5 Hz used on both sides, the spec text (EN 1991-2 5.7) names 1.7-2.1 Hz vertical, 0.9-1.1 Hz horizontal. Owner: en1991 to settle which band the entry means.
  - EN 1996 M-60 / M-52 / M-05 / M-16: the compared quantity of the `limit`/`warn` outcomes is `capacity` (the supplied value), so the rows only prove the pass-through (`mortar` = fm_pa, `frost` = frost_class, `cover` = cover_m, `lb-avail`, `spacing` = 12 m); the demand side is not compared by the example step.
- [fixed] EN 1995 registry/evaluator key mismatches (static check `🧰️scripts/demo-values/static_inputs_check.py`, the demo cases would have reported "missing input"): `t13` read `f_c_90` while the registry, fixture and oracle say `f_c90` (a force in N, relabelled); T-62 registry listed `z1`,`z3` but `parts_from` reads `h1..h3` (registry now `h1,h2,h3`, vocabulary rows z1/z3 removed); T-76/T-80/T-81/T-82 read `kmod` without listing it (added); T-50 read `members` as a number while the vocabulary `members` is the steel/timber choice of T-80 (T-50 now reads `n_members`, new vocabulary row); fixture case 13 of T-90 lacked `m_d`.
- [fixed] EN 1995 vocabulary units: `load_g`, `load_q` (N/m -> N/mm, the kernel works in N and mm), `p_d` (N/m -> N/mm); `i_tot`, `i1` (T-93) are radii of gyration in mm (second moment rows i1 kept for T-62/T-90); `f_c90` a force in N. Demo values regenerated (`demo_gen.py en1995`: 480 value rows). EN 1996: the `exposure` choices were `xo/xf/xs` while the kernel reads `mx1..mx5` (vocabulary and demo corrected). EN 1991: demo `north_german_lowland` 1.0 (E13.09 returns an error for 0 on the DE annex).
- [announce] catalogue-example rows now accept vector inputs: `name.i=value` numbers are grouped into vectors by the Python oracle adapters and by the EN 1991 / EN 1996 Rust catalogue adapters (`with_v`), rows added for E12.10, E16.06, E17.07, M-21, M-42.

## from: post-build-pipeline (wave 2)
- [done] post-build -> INFRA v4 (owner of `runner-next.sh`): add ONE hook, no other change. In `run_one`, directly after `save_state "$dir"` and the `log "done ..."` line, for artifact dirs only (`build-en19[0-9][0-9]`) add: `case "$dir" in build-en*) ( "$RUNNER/post-build.sh" "$dir" >> "$RUNNER/post-build.out" 2>&1 ) ;; esac`. The script is idempotent and state-driven (reads `build-enXXXX.env`, writes `post-enXXXX.env`); it appends `- ready for test: post-bless ...` to `build-enXXXX/latest.md` after a bless pass, which your `urgent()/decide()` already turns into the re-test. Keep the hook inside the job subshell so the runner loop is not blocked, and keep the 2-concurrent limit: the script gates on `free -m` itself but does not count toward `running_count`, so wait for it (it runs inside the job) before the slot is released. Details: `📌️important/post-build.md`. Resolved: 📌️important/post-build.md (delivered).
- [done by regen-tests] post-build -> en1997 + en1991 owners: the regen tests still WRITE `📦️.pack.semio` (en1997 `🧬️schema/📸️snapshot/🧪️tests/🔬️regen-assets/🦀️.rs`, en1991 `💡️inferences/🧪️tests/🔬️compliance-report/🦀️.rs`), but asset-laws renamed the committed files to `🎒️.pack.semio`. A bless pass would create a second pack next to each DSL and fail `at most one pack per example`. Change the file name to `🎒️.pack.semio` before the first bless.
- [done by regen-tests] post-build -> en1990/en1992/en1993/en1994/en1996 owners: no pack regen test exists (only en1991, en1995, en1997, en1998 via `SEMIO_UPDATE_GOLDEN`, en1999). Without one a stale pack can only be fixed by hand; add `regen_example_assets_when_env_set` like en1999 (`encode_en19xx_pack(decode_en19xx_dsl(dsl))`, env `SEMIO_REGEN_EN19XX_ASSETS=1`), then add the env to `bless_env()` in `post-build.sh`.

## en1995 v8 (2026-11-03)

## from: i18n-3 (wave 2 i18n audit, 2026-11-03)
Scope: `✏️s/🔌️plugins/📕️norm` Eurocode artifacts plus shared crates. Method and counts: `🔍️audits/wave2/i18n-3.md`. Language purity is clean (0 DE strings with English function words, 0 identical EN/DE pairs outside cognates), and there is no `unwrap_or(en)` fallback. The items below are EN-only text in sinks that have no German slot. Each needs a typed bilingual value, so none was edited in this pass.
- [open] en1997 -> gate reasons: 41 `gate(cond, "EN reason")?` calls in `⚖️checks/📇️catalogue/{🧗️wall-sections,⚓️walls-and-anchors,🔬️investigation,🪵️piles,💧hydraulics}` and `📐️geotechnical-parts`. `gate(ok, reason: &str) -> Result<(), String>` drops the reason into the check result. Make the reason `LocalizedCopy` or give `gate` a `(en, de)` signature. Example: `wall-sections/🦀️.rs:25` "base width must exceed toe length plus stem thickness".
- [open] en1997 (38), en1994 (52), en1993 (42), en1996 (31), en1998 (28), en1999 (19), en1991 (21) -> spec-trace descriptions: `SpecTrace::new(id, section, collection, Realisation, "EN description")` has one EN-only `&str`. Separately, 413 spec-trace rows pass an empty description (`""`) in both languages: en1992 149, en1993 93, en1995 68, en1991 47, en1990 29, en1999 21, en1996 6. Confirm whether the UI renders these rows; if it does, they show blank text.

## trace strict (wave 2, 2026-11-03, report `🔍️audits/wave2/trace-strict.md`)
- [announce] trace-strict -> shared/all: the static spec-trace verifier (`⚖️compliance/🧭️spec-trace/🧰️codegen/🐍️.py`) is catalogue-aware. A prefix fragment `enXXXX.<entry>.` (or `<entry>.`) is accepted only if the entry is in the `Entry`/`entry_of` tables of the artifact's check crates and the `format!("enXXXX.{}.…", e.id.to_ascii_lowercase()` bridge plus `CaseCatalogue for` are present; the keys after the entry must be quoted literals. Sources exclude scene, editor, viewer, io, assets, examples, field-meta, demo values, oracles, tests, mutations and spec-trace; non-catalogue fragments need their tokens inside string literals of ONE file. All ten codes ok.
- [announce] trace-strict: new test per artifact (en1992, en1995, en1996, en1997) asserting that the demo documents (without catalogue seeds) emit every catalogue-prefix trace row: `🧭️spec-trace` test files, test `every_catalogue_prefix_row_is_emitted_by_the_demo_documents`.
- [done] i18n-fix -> all norm owners and shared owner (2026-11-03, no build run, `- ready for rebuild: i18n fix` appended to integrate and build-en1990..en1999): (1) spec-trace `note` and not-modelled `reason` are `SpecText {en, de}` (schema `Text`, codegen `text_rust`, twins regenerated for all ten artifacts, 263 translated + 420 new descriptions, `spec_trace_table` renders the active locale, column id `description`); (2) `designator()`/`annex()`/`ClauseId::text(locale)`/`locator_text` in compliance core render `Annex`/`Table`/`Design case` as `Anhang`/`Tabelle`/`Bemessungsfall`, `clause_text` uses it, the 24 NDP locators are now `Annex B`; (3) 8 missing gate rules in `🗣️reasons` (en1997 gates all coded now), `Outcome.steps_de`/`.steps_de()` in en1990/en1991/en1996 (en1992 had it) with the prose steps translated, `MechanicsError::Invalid(MechanicsText)` built by `MechanicsError::invalid(en, de)`, `ui_error` message bilingual, `en1993 storey-checks` DE explanation no longer prints the EN source; clause parts that were prose (`"accidental construction loads"` etc.) are now section numbers; (4) `Lang` has no default, `Lang::of(locale)` replaces the three `if locale == De {De} else {En}` sites; (5) repo test `⚖️compliance/🧪️tests/🌐️i18n-sinks/🐍️.py` (`python3 -I`) fails on any EN-only sink. Rule for owners: new `.steps(..)` prose needs `.steps_de(..)`, new gate reasons need a rule in `🗣️reasons`, new spec-trace rows need `en` and `de` in the JSON.
- [info] i18n-fix -> en1995 owner: the en1995 descriptions were written from spec headings, clauses and realisation only; please review. `🧰️codegen/🐍️.py` still reports en1995 fragments `t-30.tapered_edge`, `t-32.apex_tension90`, `t-33.apex_reinforcement`, `t-46.notch_reinforcement` as not in the sources (pre-existing).

- [announce] SCHEMA-CODEGEN v2 -> ALL norm owners and shared owner: at **03:31** I run ONE batch over `✏️s/🔌️plugins/📕️norm` (niced, a few seconds): `schema twins annotate` (harvests the Rust structs of artifact/snapshot/diff/shared records into the JSON Schema: `x-semio-rust`, `x-semio-dsl`, `x-semio-state`), then `schema twins generate` (rewrites every twin: `🟦️.ts`, `🛰️.proto`, `🔗️.graphql` and the Rust structs of mutation leaves and annotated records). Generator output is canonical from then on. Please do NOT edit any `🟦️.ts`, `🛰️.proto`, `🔗️.graphql` twin or any `pub struct` of a root/snapshot/diff/leaf `🦀️.rs` between 03:26 and 03:35, and finish a half-done JSON+Rust edit before 03:31 (a record whose JSON and Rust struct disagree is NOT harvested and stays hand-written, but its TS/proto/GraphQL twins are regenerated from the JSON). Afterwards: edit the `🔣️.json` and run `repo ./📜️script.ts schema twins generate --under <artifact dir>` (field-adder / new_collection tools do it for you); the bun test `schema sync` is strict (committed twin == generated twin).

- [w3-thirdparty 17:00] [open] en1997 owner: GI-02 CPT behaviour index normalises Q = (q_t - sigma_v0)/sigma'_v0 (n = 1, no cap); groundhog implements the Robertson and Wride 1998 / Zhang exponent n = 0.381 I_c + 0.05 sigma'_v0/p_a - 0.15 (n <= 1) and caps C_n at 1.7 (docstring cites the Fugro PCPT guidance). Agreement < 0.1 % for sigma'_v0 >= 60 kPa in clays, 7.1 % apart at sigma'_v0 = 30 kPa (case `en1997.2.cpt-behaviour-index-4`, KnownDifference). Sandy soils at sigma'_v0 != 100 kPa are not covered (n < 1). Decide on the source of the normalisation (EN 1997-2 has no normative I_c) and either adopt the iteration or label GI-02 as the n = 1 simplification.
- [w3-thirdparty 17:00] [open] en1997 owner: GI-06 caps C_N = sqrt(p_a/sigma'_v0) at 1.7; groundhog `overburdencorrection_spt_liaowhitman` has no cap (Liao and Whitman 1986, C_N = 2.0 at 25 kPa). Case `en1997.2.spt-correction-6` (KnownDifference). The 1.7 cap is the liquefaction-practice cap (Youd 2001 / Robertson and Wride), not part of EN 1997-2 Annex F as far as the repo shows; name the source in the entry or drop the cap.
